ties being resolved by the greatest identifier, though the shipped data has no 
actual ties to worry about anyway.

Verifying the grants output structure matches spec across all branches — claims 
branch only includes positive-weight beneficiaries, the LR branch includes all 
positive shares, and the all-below-minimum branch returns zero for everyone, all
sorted ascending by beneficiary_id. I also confirm grant_allocated_cents totals 
sum correctly across regions, and consider performance: the DFS approach is fine
given the stated limits on offers per site and sites observed, though a larger 
extract with more sites per event-region would benefit from the feasibility 
pruning already in place.

I'm weighing whether to add a safety cap for unusually large groups, but I'd 
rather avoid the added complexity and risk since the schema's 120-second runtime
guarantee implies the generator already keeps groups small. Still, I worry about
a worst case — say 20 sites in one event-region — since the DFS could blow up 
exponentially; the actual data seems to spread 20 site rows across 4 regions per
event, which is a structural property I'm relying on rather than enforcing in 
code. I'm considering a cheap mitigation like ordering sites by tightness to 
help pruning fire earlier, without changing the exactness of the result.

That confirms 219k portfolios is well within the 120s budget even if the real 
extract is much bigger, so I don't need to optimize search order further. 
Reconsidering the trickiest detail: since `[export]` belongs to the export tool 
and carries no programme rule, an empty `waiver_signed` field must not be 
treated as 'Y' — that's a deliberate trap, so I'll treat empty as not satisfying
the waiver condition.

My synthetic test confirms this: EV-A SITE1 with pending status, Y signed, NONE 
waiver but empty something correctly resolves to CERTIFICATE_PENDING. I'm 
checking the sensitivity of this decision — how many rows would flip if empty 
were treated as Y instead — but since the schema explicitly specifies the rule, 
this is just informational, not something I need to second-guess further.

I'm also verifying orphan offers from offers.csv are correctly kept and occupy 
the right duplicate-detection slot, that the duplicate rule properly requires an
earlier *kept* row (not just any row) to match on offer_id and interval, and 
that the row count for profiles.tsv covers all data rows. Checking region file 
intervals, target_ticks correctly pulls from event-region targets even when no 
explicit targets row exists.

import json, os, collections

OUT = "/app/out"
names = sorted(f for f in os.listdir(OUT) if f.endswith(".json"))
print("files:", names)
docs = {}
problems = []
for name in names:
    text = open(os.path.join(OUT, name)).read()
    assert text.endswith("\n") and not text.endswith("\n\n"), name
    pairs = json.loads(text, object_pairs_hook=lambda p: p)

    def walk(node, path):
        if isinstance(node, list):
            if node and isinstance(node[0], tuple) and len(node[0]) == 2 and 
isinstance(node[0][0], str):
                keys = [k for k, _ in node]
                if keys != sorted(keys):
                    problems.append("unsorted keys at %s%s" % (name, path))
                for k, v in node:
                    walk(v, path + "/" + k)
            else:
                for i, v in enumerate(node):
                    walk(v, "%s/%d" % (path, i))
        elif isinstance(node, bool):
            problems.append("bool at %s%s" % (name, path))
        elif isinstance(node, float):
            problems.append("float at %s%s" % (name, path))
    walk(pairs, "")
    docs[name] = json.loads(text)
print("key/number problems:", problems)

summary = docs["summary.json"]
regions = [n for n in names if n.startswith("region_")]
segments = [n for n in names if n.startswith("segment_")]

# summary vs region files
for key in ("candidate_offer_count", "selected_offer_count", "relief_ticks",
            "recovery_ticks", "shortfall_ticks", "overdelivery_ticks",
            "bid_cost_cents", "credit_cents", "grant_pool_cents",
            "grant_funded_cents", "grant_allocated_cents", 
"grant_unallocated_cents"):
    total = sum(docs[n]["totals"][key] for n in regions)
    assert total == summary[key], (key, total, summary[key])
# summary vs segment files
for key in ("selected_offer_count", "relief_ticks", "recovery_ticks",
            "bid_cost_cents", "credit_cents"):
    total = sum(docs[n]["totals"][key] for n in segments)
    assert total == summary[key], ("segment", key, total, summary[key])
print("summary matches region and segment files")

# region internals
for n in regions:
    doc = docs[n]
    t = doc["totals"]
    assert t["event_count"] == len(doc["events"])
    assert t["relief_ticks"] == sum(i["relief_ticks"] for e in doc["events"] for
i in e["intervals"])
    assert t["grant_allocated_cents"] == sum(e["grant_allocated_cents"] for e in
doc["events"])
    for e in doc["events"]:
        assert e["grant_funded_cents"] == e["grant_allocated_cents"] + 
e["grant_unallocated_cents"]
        assert e["grant_allocated_cents"] == sum(g["cents"] for g in 
e["grants"])
        assert [g["beneficiary_id"] for g in e["grants"]] == 
sorted(g["beneficiary_id"] for g in e["grants"])
        assert e["selected_offer_ids"] == sorted(e["selected_offer_ids"])
        assert [i["interval"] for i in e["intervals"]] == list(range(1, 
len(e["intervals"]) + 1))
        for i in e["intervals"]:
            assert i["shortfall_ticks"] == max(0, i["target_ticks"] - 
i["relief_ticks"])
            assert i["overdelivery_ticks"] == max(0, i["relief_ticks"] - 
i["target_ticks"])
    assert [e["event_id"] for e in doc["events"]] == sorted(e["event_id"] for e 
in doc["events"])
print("region files internally consistent")

# chosen offers partition between the two segment files, per event-region
per_er = collections.defaultdict(list)
for n in segments:
    for e in docs[n]["event_regions"]:
        per_er[(e["event_id"], e["region"])].extend(e["selected_offer_ids"])
        assert e["selected_offer_ids"] == sorted(e["selected_offer_ids"])
for n in regions:
    for e in docs[n]["events"]:
        key = (e["event_id"], docs[n]["region"])
        assert sorted(per_er[key]) == e["selected_offer_ids"], key
print("segment files partition every chosen portfolio")

for n in segments:
    doc = docs[n]
    order = [(e["event_id"], e["region"]) for e in doc["event_regions"]]
    assert order == sorted(order)
    for e in doc["event_regions"]:
        if e["floor_ticks"] == 0:
            assert e["floor_attainment_bp"] == 10000
        else:
            want = min(10000, (2 * 10000 * e["relief_ticks"] + e["floor_ticks"])
// (2 * e["floor_ticks"]))
            assert e["floor_attainment_bp"] == want
print("segment ordering and attainment verified")

pol = docs["policy_audit.json"]
assert sum(pol["compliance_counts"].values()) == summary["site_count"]
region_counts = collections.Counter()
for n in regions:
    for e in docs[n]["events"]:
        for k, v in e["compliance_counts"].items():
            region_counts[k] += v
assert dict(region_counts) == pol["compliance_counts"]
assert len(pol["site_labels"]) == summary["site_count"]
order = [(s["event_id"], s["site_id"]) for s in pol["site_labels"]]
assert order == sorted(order) and len(set(order)) == len(order)
dq = docs["data_quality.json"]
assert dq["orphan_offer_rows"] + dq["ineligible_offer_count"] + 
dq["allowance_blocked_offer_count"] + summary["candidate_offer_count"] == 
dq["rows"]["offers.csv"]
print("policy audit and data quality cross-checks pass")

# Independent recomputation of credits, cap binding and grants from raw files
import json, csv, collections, configparser, xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
from fractions import Fraction
from datetime import datetime

cp = configparser.ConfigParser(); cp.read('data/program.ini')
wpt = cp.getint('units','wh_per_tick'); mpc = 
cp.getint('units','millicents_per_cent')
rc = dict(cp['region_codes']); sc = dict(cp['segment_codes'])
adder = {k:int(v) for k,v in cp['segment_rate_adders'].items()}
claim = cp.getint('grants','claim_cents_per_weight'); mingrant = 
cp.getint('grants','minimum_grant_cents')
ev = {e['event_id']: e for e in json.load(open('data/events.json'))}
sites = {}
for r in csv.DictReader(open('data/sites.csv')):
    sites[(r['event_id'], r['site_id'])] = r
cards = {}
for c in ET.parse('data/rate_cards.xml').getroot().findall('card'):
    o = c.find('override')
    cards[(c.get('event'), c.get('site'))] = (int((c.find('base') if o is None 
else o).text), int(c.find('cap').text))
prof = collections.defaultdict(dict)
for r in csv.DictReader(open('data/profiles.tsv'), delimiter='\t'):
    m = int(r['register_multiplier'])
    prof[r['offer_id']].setdefault(int(r['interval']), (int(r['relief_wh'])*m, 
int(r['recovery_wh'])*m))
offers = {o['offer_id']: o for o in csv.DictReader(open('data/offers.csv'))}
weights = {(r['event_id'], r['site_id']): int(r['grant_weight']) for r in 
csv.DictReader(open('data/recipient_weights.tsv'), delimiter='\t')}
labels = {(s['event_id'], s['site_id']): s['label'] for s in 
json.load(open('out/policy_audit.json'))['site_labels']}

def tk(wh):
    return (2*wh + wpt)//(2*wpt)

def credit_of(offer_id):
    o = offers[offer_id]; key = (o['event_id'], o['site_id'])
    base, cap = cards[key]
    rate = base + adder.get(sites[key]['segment_code'].lower() and 
sc[sites[key]['segment_code'].lower()].lower(), 0) + 
int(ev[o['event_id']]['uplift_millicents_per_kwh'])
    binding = rate > cap
    eff = min(rate, cap)
    total = 0
    for i in range(1, ev[o['event_id']]['interval_count']+1):
        wh = prof[offer_id].get(i, (0,0))
        credited = max(0, tk(wh[0]) - tk(wh[1]))
        amount = Fraction(credited * wpt * eff, 1000*mpc)
        floor = amount.numerator // amount.denominator
        frac = amount - floor
        if frac > Fraction(1,2) or (frac == Fraction(1,2) and floor % 2):
            floor += 1
        total += floor
    return total, binding

binding_total = 0
for name in ('North','South','West','East'):
    doc = json.load(open('out/region_%s.json' % name))
    for e in doc['events']:
        got = 0
        for oid in e['selected_offer_ids']:
            c, b = credit_of(oid)
            got += c
            binding_total += b
        assert got == e['credit_cents'], (name, e['event_id'], got, 
e['credit_cents'])
        # grants
        w = collections.defaultdict(int)
        recips = 0
        for (eid, sid), row in sites.items():
            if eid != e['event_id'] or rc[row['region_code'].lower()] != name: 
continue
            if (eid, sid) not in weights: continue
            recips += 1
            gw = weights[(eid, sid)]
            if labels[(eid, sid)] == 'CONTINUITY_WAIVED': gw *= 2
            w[row['beneficiary_id']] += gw
        pos = {k: v for k, v in w.items() if v > 0}
        F = e['grant_funded_cents']
        if not pos:
            exp = {}
        else:
            cl = {k: v*claim for k, v in pos.items()}
            assert sum(cl.values()) <= F, 'largest-remainder branch reached'
            exp = cl
        assert {g['beneficiary_id']: g['cents'] for g in e['grants']} == exp, 
(name, e['event_id'])
print('credits and grants independently reproduced; cap binding =', 
binding_total)
print('audit cap_binding_count =', 
json.load(open('out/policy_audit.json'))['cap_binding_count'])
EOF [timeout: 600.0s]

Summary: Verify carry chain and allowance discipline

Reasoning:
Everything checks out independently: the per-region credit recomputation using 
exact fraction arithmetic with half-to-even rounding matches, the cap binding 
count of 383 matches, and grants all fall on the claims-paid branch as expected 
since funds far exceed claims. The synthetic test also passes with the final 
code. Now I want to double-check the carry chain for one region to make sure my 
season ordering is correct, tracing how funds accumulate from the pool plus 
unused carryover.

I should verify this relation holds across the whole season order from the 
output files using the independently derived season order from events.json, and 
also check the allowance rule—confirming for each site allowance root that 
chosen offers never exceeded what was remaining at the time of their event.



$ cd /app && python3 - <<'EOF'
import json, csv, collections
from datetime import datetime

ev = json.load(open('data/events.json'))
order = sorted(sorted((e['event_id'] for e in ev), reverse=True),
               key=lambda i: datetime.fromisoformat({e['event_id']: e for e in 
ev}[i]['dispatch_start']))
docs = {r: json.load(open('out/region_%s.json' % r)) for r in 
('North','South','West','East')}
byid = {r: {e['event_id']: e for e in d['events']} for r, d in docs.items()}
for r in docs:
    carried = 0
    for eid in order:
        e = byid[r][eid]
        want = max(0, e['grant_pool_cents'] + carried - e['credit_cents'])
        assert want == e['grant_funded_cents'], (r, eid, want, 
e['grant_funded_cents'])
        carried = e['grant_unallocated_cents']
print('carry chain follows season order for every region')

# allowance discipline
alw = {}; draws = {}
for line in open('data/site_allowances.tsv'):
    if line.startswith('#'): continue
    f = line.rstrip('\n').split('\t')
    if f[0] == 'site_id': continue
    alw[f[0]] = int(f[1]); draws[f[0]] = (f[2] if len(f) > 2 else '').strip()
def root(s):
    while draws.get(s): s = draws[s]
    return s
offers = {o['offer_id']: o for o in csv.DictReader(open('data/offers.csv'))}
prof = collections.defaultdict(lambda: collections.defaultdict(int))
seen = set()
for p in csv.DictReader(open('data/profiles.tsv'), delimiter='\t'):
    k = (p['offer_id'], p['interval'])
    if k in seen: continue
    seen.add(k)
    m = int(p['register_multiplier'])
    prof[p['offer_id']][p['interval']] += (2*int(p['relief_wh'])*m + 10)//20
tot = {oid: sum(v.values()) for oid, v in prof.items()}
drawn = collections.defaultdict(int)
for eid in order:
    picked = [o for r in docs for o in byid[r][eid]['selected_offer_ids']]
    for oid in picked:
        rt = root(offers[oid]['site_id'])
        assert tot.get(oid, 0) <= alw[rt] - drawn[rt], (eid, oid)
    for oid in picked:
        drawn[root(offers[oid]['site_id'])] += tot.get(oid, 0)
over = sum(1 for s in alw if drawn[s] > alw[s])
print('every chosen offer respected its remaining allowance; roots overdrawn in 
aggregate:', over)
EOF [timeout: 300.0s]

Message from Agent ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

All nine reports are in place. Here is my report.

## What the program does

`/app/out/derive_report.py` (standard library only, `python3 derive_report.py 
DATA_DIR OUT_DIR`) reads the whole extract, then works the season through in one
pass:

- **Parsing.** `program.ini` via `configparser` (case-insensitive options), 
`events.json`, the CSV/TSV tables, the fixed-width 
`feeder_limits.txt`/`pools.txt` by character columns, `rate_cards.xml` via 
ElementTree, `site_state.jsonl` line by line, and the comment-headed 
`site_allowances.tsv`/`export_manifest.tsv`. Every record is matched on 
**region/segment names**, never codes; unknown-region `sites.csv` rows are 
counted and their state, waiver, card and weight lines dropped. Every Wh figure 
is turned into ticks (half up) at the moment it is derived, with 
`register_multiplier` applied first; the three profile skip-kinds are tested in 
the file order the contract gives.
- **Labels & eligibility.** One label per site row by the ranking in 2.6 (a 
waiver is valid only when `VALID` + signed `Y` + critical + `public`; the 
`[export]` default is ignored, so an unrecorded signature is not `Y`). Dispatch 
needs `dispatch_authorized`.
- **Season order.** Events are ordered by `dispatch_start` instant (offsets 
respected), ties by descending event id. Allowance roots follow `draws_from` 
chains; what remains of an allowance is frozen when an event is reached, so 
offers too large for it are not candidates.
- **Optimisation.** For each event-region every portfolio (at most one offer per
site, empty allowed) is enumerated depth-first with monotone ceiling pruning, 
and compared on the five components in order; remaining ties go to the greatest 
sorted offer-id tuple, and the number of portfolios tied at the best value is 
recorded.
- **Settlement & grants.** Rate = base (or `override`) + segment adder + event 
uplift, capped last; credit per interval is half-to-even on credited relief. 
Then the funded pool `F = pool + carried − credits` (carried = the previous 
event's unallocated cents for that region), claims-paid branch, otherwise 
largest remainder with the minimum-grant re-share, residual cents and residual 
ties.
- All arithmetic is integer; output is written with `sort_keys`, a trailing 
newline and the ordering each section prescribes.

## How I checked it

1. **Parser integrity:** every record count and every `field_sum` in 
`export_manifest.tsv` (bid costs, relief/recovery Wh, targets, weights, 
allowances, ceilings, pools, floors, bases) reproduces exactly — this validates 
the fixed-width column offsets in particular.
2. **Optimiser:** an independent brute force over `itertools.product` of all 
candidate combinations (no pruning, separate scoring code) agreed with the 
program on all 2000 event-regions — 0 mismatches and the same 0 tied groups.
3. **Settlement/grants:** credits recomputed per chosen offer with `Fraction` 
arithmetic and independent half-to-even rounding matched every event-region; 
cap-binding count matched (383); every grant list matched; the carry chain `F = 
pool + carried − credits` was re-verified against an independently derived 
season order; no chosen offer ever exceeded its remaining allowance.
4. **Units/paths the shipped data never reaches:** unit checks on 
`half_up`/`half_even` and on `apportion` (claims paid, largest remainder with a 
residual tie, minimum-grant re-share, all-below-minimum, zero/absent weights), 
plus a hand-built synthetic extract whose ~40 expected values I computed by hand
— it exercises ties (tie decision picked the greater offer id), the snowballing 
carry, an `override` + cap-bound rate, an exact `.5` credit going to even, a 
shared `draws_from` allowance that blocks one offer, unknown-region skipping, 
orphan/duplicate/out-of-range profile rows, a region with no site rows, and a 
non-trivial floor attainment (6667 bp). All passed.
5. **Output shape:** all nine files parse, keys are sorted at every level, each 
ends with exactly one newline, no floats or booleans anywhere, summary totals 
equal the sums over both the region files and the segment files, the segment 
files partition each chosen portfolio, orderings are as specified, and `orphans 
+ ineligible + allowance-blocked + candidates = offers.csv` rows.

Run time on the shipped extract is about 1 second.
